# ADR-0001 — Deterministic engine, LLM narrator

- **Status:** accepted (founding)
- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Provenance:** UNCONFIRMED — drafted by `agent:opus-founder` during a hands-off founding. Not human-signed.

## Context

The founding paragraph asks for an LLM that "acts like a game master" and
"move[s] the game forward". The tempting reading is to let the model hold the
game: give it the case file, let it adjudicate suggestions, let it decide who
refutes with what.

That reading fails on two counts. First, Clue is a closed-world deduction
puzzle — a model that hallucinates one refutation destroys the whole logical
chain a player has built, and the player has no way to detect it. Second, it
makes the game untestable: there is no assertion to write about a turn whose
outcome is a sampled token sequence.

The rules themselves are small and completely decidable: deal, legal move,
suggestion validity, clockwise refutation search, accusation check against a
hidden triple. Nothing in them needs a language model.

## Decision

Split the system in two, with the seam at authority:

- A **deterministic engine** owns all game state and every rule outcome. It
  holds the case file, deals hands, validates and applies moves, runs
  refutation in clockwise order, adjudicates accusations, and declares
  win/loss. It is pure logic over an explicit state value, with no network
  calls in it.
- The **LLM game master** receives engine-decided facts and produces prose:
  the scenario at setup, narration for each event, and in-fiction answers to
  player questions. It is told what happened. It never determines what
  happened.

No branch in the engine reads LLM output. Nothing is parsed out of model text
to drive state. The game is fully playable with the LLM stubbed out or failing.

## Consequences

- The engine is unit-testable without a network or an API key, and a scripted
  playthrough can assert exact outcomes.
- LLM failure degrades presentation only: on an error or empty reply, the
  engine's plain description of the event is shown and play continues.
- The game master cannot be given the case file — leaking it into a prompt
  would let narration spoil the puzzle. Prompts carry only what the receiving
  player is entitled to know.
- LLM-driven suspects making suggestions is a constrained case, not an
  exception: the model may propose, but the engine validates and can reject or
  substitute a legal action. A model's suggestion is an input, not an outcome.
- Some flavor is lost — the game master cannot invent rule twists mid-play.
  That is the point of the trade.
# ADR-0002 — OpenAI-compatible client, Poe as the configured provider

- **Status:** accepted (founding)
- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Provenance:** UNCONFIRMED — drafted by `agent:opus-founder` during a hands-off founding. Not human-signed.

## Context

The founding paragraph names the LLM access shape loosely — "api key and
endpoint, or oauth token" — and adds a purpose beyond play: the project is a
test that "we are using different agents, llms, etc and that the token cost is
shared and such". So the client is not incidental plumbing; measuring and
attributing token cost is part of what the project exists to demonstrate.

Vendor SDKs would each impose their own request shape, auth handling, and
usage reporting, which is precisely the variance that makes cost comparison
across providers hard. The OpenAI chat-completions protocol is the common
denominator that Poe and most other hosts already speak.

## Decision

Write one small HTTP client against the OpenAI-compatible chat-completions
protocol and nothing else:

- `POST <base>/chat/completions`, `Authorization: Bearer <key>`, body of
  `{model, messages, ...}`, reply read from `choices[0].message.content`.
- Base URL and model are configuration, defaulting to
  `https://api.poe.com/v1`.
- The key is `POE_API_KEY`, loaded from a gitignored `.env.local`. Never
  committed, never logged, never placed in nahel state.
- Every call returns the response's `usage` object alongside the text —
  `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `total_tokens` — and the session sums
  them. Cost is read from the provider's own numbers, never estimated from
  string length.

No vendor SDK is taken as a dependency. OAuth-token auth, mentioned in the
paragraph as an alternative, is out of scope for v1: a bearer key satisfies
the same header and is what Poe takes.

## Consequences

- Switching provider is a base-URL and model change plus a different key name;
  no code path is provider-specific.
- Usage accounting is uniform across providers, which is what makes the
  "token cost is shared" claim checkable rather than asserted.
- Provider-specific features (structured outputs, tool calling dialects,
  streaming extensions) are unavailable unless they are expressible in the
  common protocol. Given ADR-0001 — the model only narrates — this costs
  nothing today.
- A provider that diverges from the protocol is simply unsupported in v1, per
  the recorded non-goal.
# ADR-0003 — CLI-first user interface

- **Status:** accepted (founding)
- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Provenance:** UNCONFIRMED — drafted by `agent:opus-founder` during a hands-off founding. Not human-signed.

## Context

The founding paragraph calls for "a little" game and "a simple murder mystery
clue like game" whose real purpose is to exercise nahel's multi-agent,
multi-LLM, shared-cost machinery. The interface is therefore overhead against
the actual goal: every hour spent on rendering a board is an hour not spent on
the thing being tested.

A terminal interface also happens to be what an agent can drive. A scripted
playthrough that feeds stdin and asserts on stdout is a real end-to-end test;
a browser UI would need a driver, a server, and a harness before the first
assertion.

## Decision

The product surface is a terminal CLI, launched with `bun run src/cli.ts`.

- Game state is rendered as text: the player's hand, the room they occupy, the
  public event log, and their deduction notes.
- Input is a small command vocabulary — move, suggest, accuse, ask (an
  in-fiction question routed to the game master), notes, save, quit.
- Every command's effect is decided by the engine (ADR-0001); the CLI is a
  reader and a printer.
- Narration from the game master is displayed inline with the engine's own
  description of the same event available as the fallback.

No GUI, no web frontend, no TUI framework in v1.

## Consequences

- The whole game is scriptable, so an end-to-end test is a fixed command
  sequence with asserted output — no driver, no server.
- The run contract is trivially honest: `launch` is the CLI, `healthcheck` is
  `--help`, and `test` is `bun test`.
- Board geometry has to be conveyed in text. Rooms and adjacency are modelled
  as a named graph rather than a grid, which is simpler to describe out loud
  and loses nothing the rules depend on.
- If a GUI is ever wanted, the engine/CLI split means only the CLI layer is
  replaced. That is a v2 conversation and a non-goal today.

## Amendment — 2026-08-23: superseded on movement

Superseded on movement by orchestrator resolution `5rwbt08h` (journal event):
the board is a **dice-and-corridor board per the standard rule set**, not a
named graph.

The "rooms and adjacency are modelled as a named graph rather than a grid"
consequence above no longer holds. `src/engine/board.ts` builds nine room boxes
and seven corridor segments into 77 unit squares with 17 doors and two secret
passages; movement spends a rolled die across those squares, corridor squares
hold one token and block passage, and entering a room ends the move.

Everything else in this ADR stands — the surface is still a terminal CLI, the
engine still decides every outcome, and board geometry is still conveyed in
text. Only the claim about how geometry is *modelled* is withdrawn.

 succeeded in 0ms:
/**
 * The narrator: engine events in, prose out.
 *
 * One batched call per turn — a call per event would be slow, expensive, and
 * would lose the thread of what just happened. The engine's own
 * `describeEvent` sentence for each event is what goes INTO the prompt, so the
 * model is rephrasing a fact rather than inventing one, and it is also what
 * comes back out on any failure: narration is discardable by construction
 * (CONTEXT.md "Narration", ADR-0001).
 *
 * The caller decides whose events these are — pass `visibleEvents(state, id)`
 * for a player, `publicEvents(state)` for the table. Nothing here filters
 * visibility, because nothing here knows who is reading.
 */

import type { GameEvent } from '../engine/types.ts';
import { describeEvent } from '../engine/view.ts';
import type { ChatClient, ChatMessage } from '../llm/index.ts';
import type { Scenario } from './scenario.ts';

export type NarrationLine = {
  readonly event: GameEvent;
  readonly text: string;
  readonly source: 'llm' | 'fallback';
};

const MAX_LINE_LENGTH = 400;

/** The engine's own account of each event — the fallback, verbatim. */
export function fallbackNarration(events: readonly GameEvent[]): NarrationLine[] {
  return events.map((event) => ({ event, text: describeEvent(event), source: 'fallback' as const }));
}

export function narrationMessages(
  scenario: Scenario,
  events: readonly GameEvent[],
): ChatMessage[] {
  return [
    {
      role: 'system',
      content: [
        'You are the narrator of a parlour-murder mystery. You are told what happened and you say it well.',
        'Rules you may not break:',
        '- Never add an event, a clue, or a deduction that is not in the list you are given.',
        '- Never say who the murderer is, or what the murder weapon or room was. You do not know.',
        '- One sentence per numbered line, in the same order.',
        `Reply with a JSON array of ${events.length} strings and nothing else.`,
      ].join('\n'),
    },
    {
      role: 'user',
      content: [
        `Setting: ${scenario.setting}`,
        `The victim: ${scenario.victim}`,
        '',
        'What just happened, in order:',
        ...events.map((event, index) => `${index + 1}. ${describeEvent(event)}`),
      ].join('\n'),
    },
  ];
}

function unfence(text: string): string {
  const fenced = /^\s*```(?:json)?\s*\n([\s\S]*?)\n?\s*```\s*$/.exec(text);
  return fenced?.[1] ?? text;
}

/**
 * Read up to `count` narration strings from a model reply.
 *
 * Returns one slot per event: a string where the model supplied a usable one,
 * null where it did not. A reply that is not a JSON array yields all nulls, so
 * the batch falls back wholesale.
 */
export function parseNarration(text: string, count: number): (string | null)[] {
  const slots: (string | null)[] = Array.from({ length: count }, () => null);
  let payload: unknown;
  try {
    payload = JSON.parse(unfence(text));
  } catch {
    return slots;
  }
  if (!Array.isArray(payload)) return slots;

  for (let index = 0; index < count; index += 1) {
    const entry = payload[index];
    if (typeof entry !== 'string') continue;
    const line = entry.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
    if (line.length === 0) continue;
    slots[index] = line.length > MAX_LINE_LENGTH ? `${line.slice(0, MAX_LINE_LENGTH)}…` : line;
  }
  return slots;
}

export type NarrationRequest = {
  readonly scenario: Scenario;
  readonly events: readonly GameEvent[];
  /** Model for this call only; defaults to the client's configured model. */
  readonly model?: string;
};

/** Narrate a turn's events, falling back to the engine's own sentences per event. */
export async function narrateEvents(
  client: ChatClient,
  request: NarrationRequest,
): Promise<NarrationLine[]> {
  const { events } = request;
  if (events.length === 0) return [];

  const reply = await client.tryChat(narrationMessages(request.scenario, events), {
    ...(request.model === undefined ? {} : { model: request.model }),
    params: { temperature: 0.8 },
  });
  if (!reply.ok) return fallbackNarration(events);

  const slots = parseNarration(reply.value.text, events.length);
  return events.map((event, index) => {
    const line = slots[index];
    return line === null || line === undefined
      ? { event, text: describeEvent(event), source: 'fallback' as const }
      : { event, text: line, source: 'llm' as const };
  });
}
/**
 * The LLM game master.
 *
 * Scenario, narration and in-fiction Q&A, layered over engine-decided facts —
 * plus the deterministic opponent policy that plays the suspects the human is
 * not. The model describes; it never decides (ADR-0001).
 *
 * Everything goes through ONE `ChatClient`, which is what makes the session's
 * token ledger honest: the roles may run on different models, and the ledger
 * attributes each call's `usage` figures to the model that reported them
 * (PRODUCT.md hard constraint 6, orchestrator resolution 5rwbt08h).
 */

import type { Suspect } from '../engine/cards.ts';
import type { GameEvent } from '../engine/types.ts';
import type { PlayerView } from '../engine/view.ts';
import type { ChatClient, SessionUsage } from '../llm/index.ts';
import { CANNED_SCENARIO, requestScenario, type Scenario } from './scenario.ts';
import { narrateEvents, type NarrationLine } from './narrator.ts';
import { askSuspect, type SuspectAnswer } from './qa.ts';

export {
  CANNED_SCENARIO,
  parseScenario,
  requestScenario,
  scenarioMessages,
  type Scenario,
  type ScenarioOptions,
} from './scenario.ts';
export {
  fallbackNarration,
  narrateEvents,
  narrationMessages,
  parseNarration,
  type NarrationLine,
  type NarrationRequest,
} from './narrator.ts';
export {
  askSuspect,
  fallbackAnswer,
  questionMessages,
  visibleFactsFor,
  type QuestionRequest,
  type SuspectAnswer,
  type VisibleFacts,
} from './qa.ts';
export { buildNotebook, type Notebook } from './notebook.ts';
export {
  applyOpponentPlan,
  planOpponentAction,
  playOpponentTurn,
  refuteAsOpponent,
  type OpponentPlan,
} from './opponent.ts';

export type GameMasterOptions = {
  /** Per-role model overrides. Each defaults to the client's configured model. */
  readonly models?: {
    readonly scenario?: string;
    readonly narration?: string;
    readonly qa?: string;
  };
};

export class GameMaster {
  readonly client: ChatClient;
  readonly models: NonNullable<GameMasterOptions['models']>;

  #scenario: Scenario | null = null;

  constructor(client: ChatClient, options: GameMasterOptions = {}) {
    this.client = client;
    this.models = options.models ?? {};
  }

  /** The session's token ledger, straight from the shared client. */
  get usage(): SessionUsage {
    return this.client.usage;
  }

  /** The scenario opened for this session, or null before `openScenario`. */
  get scenario(): Scenario | null {
    return this.#scenario;
  }

  /**
   * Generate the session's scenario, once. Repeat calls return the same value
   * without spending another token — the fiction is fixed for the session.
   */
  async openScenario(): Promise<Scenario> {
    if (this.#scenario !== null) return this.#scenario;
    this.#scenario = await requestScenario(this.client, {
      ...(this.models.scenario === undefined ? {} : { model: this.models.scenario }),
    });
    return this.#scenario;
  }

  /** Narrate a turn's events, one batched call, falling back per event. */
  async narrate(events: readonly GameEvent[]): Promise<NarrationLine[]> {
    return narrateEvents(this.client, {
      scenario: this.#scenario ?? CANNED_SCENARIO,
      events,
      ...(this.models.narration === undefined ? {} : { model: this.models.narration }),
    });
  }

  /** Put one in-fiction question to one suspect, from the asker's view alone. */
  async ask(view: PlayerView, suspect: Suspect, question: string): Promise<SuspectAnswer> {
    return askSuspect(this.client, {
      scenario: this.#scenario ?? CANNED_SCENARIO,
      view,
      suspect,
      question,
      ...(this.models.qa === undefined ? {} : { model: this.models.qa }),
    });
  }
}

/**
 * The session's token ledger as printable lines — measured from each response's
 * own `usage` object, never estimated from string length (ADR-0002).
 *
 * Calls a provider reported no usage for are stated rather than hidden: a
 * ledger that silently drops them would understate the session.
 */
export function formatUsageLedger(usage: SessionUsage): string[] {
  if (usage.calls === 0) return ['No LLM calls were made this session.'];

  const lines = [
    `LLM usage: ${usage.calls} ${usage.calls === 1 ? 'call' : 'calls'}, ` +
      `${usage.total_tokens} tokens (${usage.prompt_tokens} prompt + ${usage.completion_tokens} completion)`,
  ];
  for (const model of Object.keys(usage.byModel).sort()) {
    const totals = usage.byModel[model];
    if (totals === undefined) continue;
    lines.push(
      `  ${model}: ${totals.calls} ${totals.calls === 1 ? 'call' : 'calls'}, ` +
        `${totals.total_tokens} tokens (${totals.prompt_tokens} prompt + ${totals.completion_tokens} completion)`,
    );
  }
  if (usage.callsWithoutUsage > 0) {
    lines.push(
      `  ${usage.callsWithoutUsage} ${usage.callsWithoutUsage === 1 ? 'call' : 'calls'} reported no usage and ${
        usage.callsWithoutUsage === 1 ? 'is' : 'are'
      } not counted above`,
    );
  }
  return lines;
}

     1	/**
     2	 * The scenario: the fiction wrapped around a mechanically standard game.
     3	 *
     4	 * One LLM call at setup produces a victim, an era and setting, an opening
     5	 * paragraph, and a one-line persona per suspect. Everything after that is
     6	 * fixed for the session (CONTEXT.md, "Scenario").
     7	 *
     8	 * PRODUCT.md hard constraint 4 and ADR-0001 govern the failure path: game
     9	 * legality never depends on LLM output, so a transport error, a reply that is
    10	 * not JSON, or JSON of the wrong shape all degrade to the canned scenario
    11	 * below — field by field where possible — and the game is played exactly the

    13	 * names are public knowledge and the case file is not in scope here.

    20	export type Scenario = {
    21	  readonly victim: string;
    22	  /** Era and place, in one phrase. */
    23	  readonly setting: string;
    24	  /** The opening paragraph read to the player once. */
    25	  readonly intro: string;
    26	  /** One line of voice per suspect — the only thing the Q&A layer role-plays from. */
    27	  readonly personas: Readonly<Record<Suspect, string>>;
    28	  readonly source: 'llm' | 'fallback';
    29	};

    31	/**
    32	 * The scenario used whenever the model is unavailable or unusable.
    33	 *
    34	 * Deliberately card-free apart from the suspects' own names: this text is fed
    35	 * into in-fiction Q&A prompts, and a canned line that named a room or a weapon
    36	 * would put a card into a prompt for no reason.
    37	 */
    38	export const CANNED_SCENARIO: Scenario = {
    39	  victim: 'Doctor Alastair Vane',
    40	  setting: 'A great house cut off by a winter storm, a few years after the war.',
    41	  intro:
    42	    'The storm took the telephone lines at dusk, and the doctor was dead before the lamps were lit. ' +
    43	    'Six guests remain under one roof, each with a reason to be elsewhere and none with a way to leave. ' +
    44	    'Someone here is lying, and the truth is small enough to fit in three words.',
    45	  personas: {
    46	    'Miss Scarlett': 'Poised and quick, and answers an awkward question with a better one.',
    47	    'Colonel Mustard': 'Bluff, military, fond of protocol, and offended that anyone would ask.',
    48	    'Mrs. White': 'Keeps the household running and her opinions to herself. Misses nothing.',
    49	    'Reverend Green': 'Soft-spoken and watchful, forever steering the talk toward forgiveness.',
    50	    'Mrs. Peacock': 'Impeccably mannered, socially ruthless, certain the whole affair is beneath her.',
    51	    'Professor Plum': 'Distracted and precise, far more interested in the puzzle than the tragedy.',
    52	  },
    53	  source: 'fallback',
    54	};
    55	
    56	const MAX_FIELD_LENGTH = 800;
    57	
    58	/** The two messages the scenario call sends. Carries no game state. */
    59	export function scenarioMessages(): ChatMessage[] {
    60	  return [
    61	    {
    62	      role: 'system',
    63	      content:
    64	        'You dress a game of parlour-murder deduction in fiction. You invent atmosphere only: ' +
    65	        'the mechanics of the game are fixed and are none of your concern. ' +
    66	        'Reply with a single JSON object and nothing else — no prose, no code fences.',
    67	    },
    68	    {
    69	      role: 'user',
    70	      content: [
    71	        'Invent a murder mystery for these six suspects:',
    72	        SUSPECTS.map((suspect) => `- ${suspect}`).join('\n'),
    73	        '',
    74	        `The house has these rooms: ${ROOMS.join(', ')}.`,
    75	        `These objects are in play: ${WEAPONS.join(', ')}.`,
    76	        '',
    77	        'Return exactly this JSON shape:',
    78	        '{"victim": "<the murdered person\'s name>",',
    79	        ' "setting": "<era and place, one phrase>",',
    80	        ' "intro": "<one paragraph read aloud at the start, 3-5 sentences>",',
    81	        ' "suspects": [{"name": "<one of the six names above, exactly>", "persona": "<one line of voice and motive>"}]',
    82	        '}',
    83	        '',
    84	        'Do not name the murderer, the murder weapon, or the murder room: nobody knows them yet, including you.',
    85	      ].join('\n'),
    86	    },
    87	  ];
    88	}
    89	
    90	/** Collapse whitespace and clamp — a model that runs away must not bloat every later prompt. */
    91	function tidy(value: unknown): string | null {
    92	  if (typeof value !== 'string') return null;
    93	  const text = value.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
    94	  if (text.length === 0) return null;
    95	  return text.length > MAX_FIELD_LENGTH ? `${text.slice(0, MAX_FIELD_LENGTH)}…` : text;
    96	}
    97	
    98	/** Strip the code fence models wrap JSON in, if there is one. */
    99	function unfence(text: string): string {
   100	  const fenced = /^\s*```(?:json)?\s*\n([\s\S]*?)\n?\s*```\s*$/.exec(text);
   101	  return fenced?.[1] ?? text;
   102	}
   103	
   104	/**
   105	 * Read a scenario out of a model reply, filling each missing or unusable field
   106	 * from the canned scenario. Returns null only when the reply is not a JSON
   107	 * object at all — there is then nothing to salvage.
   108	 */
   109	export function parseScenario(text: string): Scenario | null {
   110	  let payload: unknown;
   111	  try {
   112	    payload = JSON.parse(unfence(text));
   113	  } catch {
   114	    return null;
   115	  }
   116	  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null || Array.isArray(payload)) return null;
   117	  const record = payload as Record<string, unknown>;
   118	
   119	  const personas: Record<Suspect, string> = { ...CANNED_SCENARIO.personas };
   120	  const entries = record.suspects;
   121	  if (Array.isArray(entries)) {
   122	    for (const entry of entries) {
   123	      if (typeof entry !== 'object' || entry === null) continue;
   124	      const { name, persona } = entry as Record<string, unknown>;
   125	      const line = tidy(persona);
   126	      if (isSuspect(name) && line !== null) personas[name] = line;
   127	    }
   128	  }
   129	
   130	  return {
   131	    victim: tidy(record.victim) ?? CANNED_SCENARIO.victim,
   132	    setting: tidy(record.setting) ?? CANNED_SCENARIO.setting,
   133	    intro: tidy(record.intro) ?? CANNED_SCENARIO.intro,
   134	    personas,
   135	    source: 'llm',
   136	  };
   137	}
   138	
   139	export type ScenarioOptions = {
   140	  /** Model for this call only; defaults to the client's configured model. */
   141	  readonly model?: string;
   142	};
   143	
   144	/**
   145	 * Ask the model for one scenario, degrading to the canned one on any failure.
   146	 *
   147	 * `response_format: {type: "json_object"}` is requested because the endpoints
   148	 * that honour it produce far cleaner replies. A provider that does not know the
   149	 * parameter answers 4xx, so exactly one retry is made without it before giving
   150	 * up — a 5xx is NOT retried here, the transport already retried it once.
   151	 */
   152	export async function requestScenario(
   153	  client: ChatClient,
   154	  options: ScenarioOptions = {},
   155	): Promise<Scenario> {
   156	  const messages = scenarioMessages();
   157	  const model = options.model === undefined ? {} : { model: options.model };
   158	
   159	  const first = await client.tryChat(messages, {
   160	    ...model,
   161	    params: { temperature: 0.9, response_format: { type: 'json_object' } },
   162	  });
   163	
   164	  let reply = first;
   165	  if (!first.ok && first.error instanceof LlmHttpError && first.error.status < 500) {
   166	    reply = await client.tryChat(messages, { ...model, params: { temperature: 0.9 } });
   167	  }
   168	
   169	  if (!reply.ok) return CANNED_SCENARIO;
   170	  return parseScenario(reply.value.text) ?? CANNED_SCENARIO;
   171	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * The narrator: engine events in, prose out.
     3	 *
     4	 * One batched call per turn — a call per event would be slow, expensive, and
     5	 * would lose the thread of what just happened. The engine's own
     6	 * `describeEvent` sentence for each event is what goes INTO the prompt, so the
     7	 * model is rephrasing a fact rather than inventing one, and it is also what
     8	 * comes back out on any failure: narration is discardable by construction
     9	 * (CONTEXT.md "Narration", ADR-0001).
    10	 *
    11	 * The caller decides whose events these are — pass `visibleEvents(state, id)`
    12	 * for a player, `publicEvents(state)` for the table. Nothing here filters
    13	 * visibility, because nothing here knows who is reading.
    14	 */
    15	
    16	import type { GameEvent } from '../engine/types.ts';
    17	import { describeEvent } from '../engine/view.ts';
    18	import type { ChatClient, ChatMessage } from '../llm/index.ts';
    19	import type { Scenario } from './scenario.ts';
    20	
    21	export type NarrationLine = {
    22	  readonly event: GameEvent;
    23	  readonly text: string;
    24	  readonly source: 'llm' | 'fallback';
    25	};
    26	
    27	const MAX_LINE_LENGTH = 400;
    28	
    29	/** The engine's own account of each event — the fallback, verbatim. */
    30	export function fallbackNarration(events: readonly GameEvent[]): NarrationLine[] {
    31	  return events.map((event) => ({ event, text: describeEvent(event), source: 'fallback' as const }));
    32	}
    33	
    34	export function narrationMessages(
    35	  scenario: Scenario,
    36	  events: readonly GameEvent[],
    37	): ChatMessage[] {
    38	  return [
    39	    {
    40	      role: 'system',
    41	      content: [
    42	        'You are the narrator of a parlour-murder mystery. You are told what happened and you say it well.',
    43	        'Rules you may not break:',
    44	        '- Never add an event, a clue, or a deduction that is not in the list you are given.',
    45	        '- Never say who the murderer is, or what the murder weapon or room was. You do not know.',
    46	        '- One sentence per numbered line, in the same order.',
    47	        `Reply with a JSON array of ${events.length} strings and nothing else.`,
    48	      ].join('\n'),
    49	    },
    50	    {
    51	      role: 'user',
    52	      content: [
    53	        `Setting: ${scenario.setting}`,
    54	        `The victim: ${scenario.victim}`,
    55	        '',
    56	        'What just happened, in order:',
    57	        ...events.map((event, index) => `${index + 1}. ${describeEvent(event)}`),
    58	      ].join('\n'),
    59	    },
    60	  ];
    61	}
    62	
    63	function unfence(text: string): string {
    64	  const fenced = /^\s*```(?:json)?\s*\n([\s\S]*?)\n?\s*```\s*$/.exec(text);
    65	  return fenced?.[1] ?? text;
    66	}
    67	
    68	/**
    69	 * Read up to `count` narration strings from a model reply.
    70	 *
    71	 * Returns one slot per event: a string where the model supplied a usable one,
    72	 * null where it did not. A reply that is not a JSON array yields all nulls, so
    73	 * the batch falls back wholesale.
    74	 */
    75	export function parseNarration(text: string, count: number): (string | null)[] {
    76	  const slots: (string | null)[] = Array.from({ length: count }, () => null);
    77	  let payload: unknown;
    78	  try {
    79	    payload = JSON.parse(unfence(text));
    80	  } catch {
    81	    return slots;
    82	  }
    83	  if (!Array.isArray(payload)) return slots;
    84	
    85	  for (let index = 0; index < count; index += 1) {
    86	    const entry = payload[index];
    87	    if (typeof entry !== 'string') continue;
    88	    const line = entry.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
    89	    if (line.length === 0) continue;
    90	    slots[index] = line.length > MAX_LINE_LENGTH ? `${line.slice(0, MAX_LINE_LENGTH)}…` : line;
    91	  }
    92	  return slots;
    93	}
    94	
    95	export type NarrationRequest = {
    96	  readonly scenario: Scenario;
    97	  readonly events: readonly GameEvent[];
    98	  /** Model for this call only; defaults to the client's configured model. */
    99	  readonly model?: string;
   100	};
   101	
   102	/** Narrate a turn's events, falling back to the engine's own sentences per event. */
   103	export async function narrateEvents(
   104	  client: ChatClient,
   105	  request: NarrationRequest,
   106	): Promise<NarrationLine[]> {
   107	  const { events } = request;
   108	  if (events.length === 0) return [];
   109	
   110	  const reply = await client.tryChat(narrationMessages(request.scenario, events), {
   111	    ...(request.model === undefined ? {} : { model: request.model }),
   112	    params: { temperature: 0.8 },
   113	  });
   114	  if (!reply.ok) return fallbackNarration(events);
   115	
   116	  const slots = parseNarration(reply.value.text, events.length);
   117	  return events.map((event, index) => {
   118	    const line = slots[index];
   119	    return line === null || line === undefined
   120	      ? { event, text: describeEvent(event), source: 'fallback' as const }
   121	      : { event, text: line, source: 'llm' as const };
   122	  });
   123	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * In-fiction Q&A: the player asks one suspect one free-form question per turn,
     3	 * and the suspect answers in character.
     4	 *
     5	 * The leak is prevented by CONSTRUCTION, not by instruction. `visibleFactsFor`
     6	 * builds an explicit, enumerated bundle of facts and the prompt is assembled
     7	 * from that bundle alone — the model is never handed the `GameState`, the
     8	 * event log, any hand (including the asker's own), or the board's card
     9	 * inventory. Telling a model "do not reveal the solution" while pasting the
    10	 * solution into its context is not a safeguard; not pasting it is.
    11	 *
    12	 * What the bundle deliberately excludes, and why:
    13	 *   - the case file — nobody may see it, ever (ADR-0001);
    14	 *   - every hand, including the asker's — a suspect has no business knowing
    15	 *     which cards anyone holds, and passing them would put six card names into
    16	 *     a prompt for no gain;
    17	 *   - the event log — suggestions name arbitrary cards, so the log is the
    18	 *     single easiest way to leak the answer into a prompt;
    19	 *   - weapon and non-player token positions — same reason, less obviously.
    20	 *
    21	 * What it includes is public and already on the player's screen: the turn
    22	 * number, where the asked suspect and the asker stand, who is still in the
    23	 * running, and how many suggestions have been heard (counts, never contents).
    24	 */
    25	
    26	import type { Suspect } from '../engine/cards.ts';
    27	import { describePosition, type PlayerView } from '../engine/view.ts';
    28	import type { ChatClient, ChatMessage } from '../llm/index.ts';
    29	import type { Scenario } from './scenario.ts';
    30	
    31	export type VisibleFacts = {
    32	  readonly turnNumber: number;
    33	  readonly askedSuspect: Suspect;
    34	  /** Where that suspect's token stands, as a phrase. Public. */
    35	  readonly askedSuspectLocation: string;
    36	  readonly askerCharacter: Suspect;
    37	  readonly askerLocation: string;
    38	  /** The player-controlled tokens not yet barred from winning. Public. */
    39	  readonly stillInTheRunning: readonly Suspect[];
    40	  /** How many suggestions this seat has heard. A count — never the cards. */
    41	  readonly suggestionsHeard: number;
    42	  readonly unrefutedSuggestionsHeard: number;
    43	};
    44	
    45	export type SuspectAnswer = {
    46	  readonly suspect: Suspect;
    47	  readonly text: string;
    48	  readonly source: 'llm' | 'fallback';
    49	};
    50	
    51	const MAX_ANSWER_LENGTH = 600;
    52	const MAX_QUESTION_LENGTH = 400;
    53	
    54	/** The whole fact set a suspect is told about. Nothing else reaches the prompt. */
    55	export function visibleFactsFor(view: PlayerView, suspect: Suspect): VisibleFacts {
    56	  const seats: Suspect[] = [
    57	    ...(view.eliminated ? [] : [view.character]),
    58	    ...view.opponents.filter((opponent) => !opponent.eliminated).map((opponent) => opponent.character),
    59	  ];
    60	  let suggestionsHeard = 0;
    61	  let unrefutedSuggestionsHeard = 0;
    62	  for (const event of view.events) {
    63	    if (event.type === 'suggestion-made') suggestionsHeard += 1;
    64	    if (event.type === 'suggestion-unrefuted') unrefutedSuggestionsHeard += 1;
    65	  }
    66	
    67	  return {
    68	    turnNumber: view.turnNumber,
    69	    askedSuspect: suspect,
    70	    askedSuspectLocation: describePosition(view.suspectPositions[suspect]),
    71	    askerCharacter: view.character,
    72	    askerLocation: describePosition(view.position),
    73	    stillInTheRunning: seats,
    74	    suggestionsHeard,
    75	    unrefutedSuggestionsHeard,
    76	  };
    77	}
    78	
    79	function clamp(text: string, limit: number): string {
    80	  const tidy = text.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
    81	  return tidy.length > limit ? `${tidy.slice(0, limit)}…` : tidy;
    82	}
    83	
    84	export function questionMessages(
    85	  scenario: Scenario,
    86	  suspect: Suspect,
    87	  question: string,
    88	  facts: VisibleFacts,
    89	): ChatMessage[] {
    90	  return [
    91	    {
    92	      role: 'system',
    93	      content: [
    94	        `You are ${suspect}, a guest questioned about a death in the house.`,
    95	        `Your character: ${scenario.personas[suspect]}`,
    96	        `Setting: ${scenario.setting}`,
    97	        `The dead: ${scenario.victim}`,
    98	        '',
    99	        'Answer in character, in one or two sentences, in the first person.',
   100	        'You are being questioned, not confessing: evade, deflect, or bristle as your character would.',
   101	        'You do not know who the murderer is, what was used, or where it happened — nobody does yet.',
   102	        'Never invent a clue, a location, an object, or another guest\'s whereabouts.',
   103	        'You know only the facts listed by the questioner. Say nothing beyond them.',
   104	      ].join('\n'),
   105	    },
   106	    {
   107	      role: 'user',
   108	      content: [
   109	        'Facts you both can see:',
   110	        `- It is turn ${facts.turnNumber}.`,
   111	        `- You are in ${facts.askedSuspectLocation}.`,
   112	        `- ${facts.askerCharacter}, who is asking, is in ${facts.askerLocation}.`,
   113	        `- Still under suspicion: ${facts.stillInTheRunning.join(', ')}.`,
   114	        `- Accusations aired so far: ${facts.suggestionsHeard}, of which ${facts.unrefutedSuggestionsHeard} went unanswered.`,
   115	        '',
   116	        `${facts.askerCharacter} asks you: ${clamp(question, MAX_QUESTION_LENGTH)}`,
   117	      ].join('\n'),
   118	    },
   119	  ];
   120	}
   121	
   122	/** What a suspect says when the model is unavailable. In character, and empty of information. */
   123	export function fallbackAnswer(suspect: Suspect): SuspectAnswer {
   124	  return {
   125	    suspect,
   126	    text: `${suspect} holds your eye for a moment, says nothing worth writing down, and turns away.`,
   127	    source: 'fallback',
   128	  };
   129	}
   130	
   131	export type QuestionRequest = {
   132	  readonly scenario: Scenario;
   133	  /** The ASKER's view. Only the whitelist in `visibleFactsFor` is used. */
   134	  readonly view: PlayerView;
   135	  readonly suspect: Suspect;
   136	  readonly question: string;
   137	  /** Model for this call only; defaults to the client's configured model. */
   138	  readonly model?: string;
   139	};
   140	
   141	/** Put one question to one suspect. Never throws; an unusable reply is no answer. */
   142	export async function askSuspect(
   143	  client: ChatClient,
   144	  request: QuestionRequest,
   145	): Promise<SuspectAnswer> {
   146	  const facts = visibleFactsFor(request.view, request.suspect);
   147	  const reply = await client.tryChat(
   148	    questionMessages(request.scenario, request.suspect, request.question, facts),
   149	    {
   150	      ...(request.model === undefined ? {} : { model: request.model }),
   151	      params: { temperature: 0.9 },
   152	    },
   153	  );
   154	  if (!reply.ok) return fallbackAnswer(request.suspect);
   155	
   156	  const text = clamp(reply.value.text, MAX_ANSWER_LENGTH);
   157	  if (text.length === 0) return fallbackAnswer(request.suspect);
   158	  return { suspect: request.suspect, text, source: 'llm' };
   159	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * Projections of game state for consumers that must NOT see everything.
     3	 *
     4	 * The CLI and the LLM game master are both untrusted with the case file and
     5	 * with other players' hands (ADR-0001: leaking either into a prompt would spoil
     6	 * the puzzle). They read a `PlayerView`, never `GameState`.
     7	 *
     8	 * `describeEvent` is also the narration fallback: when the game master returns
     9	 * nothing or errors, the engine's own plain sentence is shown instead.
    10	 */
    11	
    12	import type { Card, Room, Suspect, Weapon } from './cards.ts';
    13	import type { Position } from './board.ts';
    14	import type { GameEvent, GameState, PlayerId, TurnPhase } from './types.ts';
    15	import { IllegalActionError } from './types.ts';
    16	
    17	/** Events a given player is entitled to see, in order. */
    18	export function visibleEvents(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): GameEvent[] {
    19	  return state.events.filter(
    20	    (event) => event.visibleTo === 'all' || event.visibleTo.includes(playerId),
    21	  );
    22	}
    23	
    24	/** Events every player can see. Safe to show to a spectator or a narrator. */
    25	export function publicEvents(state: GameState): GameEvent[] {
    26	  return state.events.filter((event) => event.visibleTo === 'all');
    27	}
    28	
    29	/** The cards a player holds. Throws for an unknown player. */
    30	export function handOf(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): readonly Card[] {
    31	  const player = state.players.find((candidate) => candidate.id === playerId);
    32	  if (!player) throw new IllegalActionError(`no such player: ${playerId}`);
    33	  return player.hand;
    34	}
    35	
    36	export type PlayerView = {
    37	  readonly you: PlayerId;
    38	  readonly character: Suspect;
    39	  readonly hand: readonly Card[];
    40	  readonly position: Position;
    41	  readonly eliminated: boolean;
    42	  readonly phase: TurnPhase;
    43	  readonly roll: number | null;
    44	  readonly currentPlayer: PlayerId;
    45	  readonly yourTurn: boolean;
    46	  readonly turnNumber: number;
    47	  readonly over: boolean;
    48	  readonly winner: PlayerId | null;
    49	  /** Public facts about the other seats: no hands, only hand SIZES. */
    50	  readonly opponents: readonly {
    51	    readonly id: PlayerId;
    52	    readonly character: Suspect;
    53	    readonly handSize: number;
    54	    readonly eliminated: boolean;
    55	    readonly position: Position;
    56	  }[];
    57	  readonly suspectPositions: Readonly<Record<Suspect, Position>>;
    58	  readonly weaponPositions: Readonly<Record<Weapon, Room>>;
    59	  readonly events: readonly GameEvent[];
    60	  /**
    61	   * The refutation the game is waiting on, or null. Everyone may know WHO owes
    62	   * the choice — it is why play has stopped — but the cards on offer are part
    63	   * of the refuter's hand, so `options` is filled in for that player alone.
    64	   */
    65	  readonly pendingRefutation: {
    66	    readonly suggester: PlayerId;
    67	    readonly refuter: PlayerId;
    68	    readonly yours: boolean;
    69	    readonly options: readonly Card[] | null;
    70	  } | null;
    71	};
    72	
    73	/**
    74	 * Everything one player may know: their own hand, public board state, and the
    75	 * events addressed to them. The case file is absent by construction, and so is
    76	 * every other hand.
    77	 */
    78	export function playerView(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): PlayerView {
    79	  const player = state.players.find((candidate) => candidate.id === playerId);
    80	  if (!player) throw new IllegalActionError(`no such player: ${playerId}`);
    81	  const current = state.players[state.currentPlayerIndex];
    82	  return {
    83	    you: player.id,
    84	    character: player.character,
    85	    hand: player.hand,
    86	    position: state.suspectPositions[player.character],
    87	    eliminated: player.eliminated,
    88	    phase: state.phase,
    89	    roll: state.roll,
    90	    currentPlayer: current?.id ?? player.id,
    91	    yourTurn: current?.id === player.id,
    92	    turnNumber: state.turnNumber,
    93	    over: state.over,
    94	    winner: state.winner,
    95	    opponents: state.players
    96	      .filter((candidate) => candidate.id !== playerId)
    97	      .map((candidate) => ({
    98	        id: candidate.id,
    99	        character: candidate.character,
   100	        handSize: candidate.hand.length,
   101	        eliminated: candidate.eliminated,
   102	        position: state.suspectPositions[candidate.character],
   103	      })),
   104	    suspectPositions: state.suspectPositions,
   105	    weaponPositions: state.weaponPositions,
   106	    events: visibleEvents(state, playerId),
   107	    pendingRefutation:
   108	      state.pendingRefutation === null
   109	        ? null
   110	        : {
   111	            suggester: state.pendingRefutation.suggester,
   112	            refuter: state.pendingRefutation.refuter,
   113	            yours: state.pendingRefutation.refuter === playerId,
   114	            options:
   115	              state.pendingRefutation.refuter === playerId ? state.pendingRefutation.options : null,
   116	          },
   117	  };
   118	}
   119	
   120	/** A position as a short human phrase, e.g. "the Library" or "corridor 6,7". */
   121	export function describePosition(position: Position): string {
   122	  return position.kind === 'room' ? `the ${position.room}` : `corridor ${position.x},${position.y}`;
   123	}
   124	
   125	/** One event as a plain English sentence — the narration fallback. */
   126	export function describeEvent(event: GameEvent): string {
   127	  switch (event.type) {
   128	    case 'game-started':
   129	      return `The game begins with ${event.players
   130	        .map((entry) => `${entry.id} as ${entry.character}`)
   131	        .join(', ')}.`;
   132	    case 'turn-started':
   133	      return `Turn ${event.turn}: it is ${event.player}'s move.`;
   134	    case 'rolled':
   135	      return `${event.player} rolls a ${event.value}.`;
   136	    case 'moved':
   137	      return `${event.player} moves ${event.steps} ${
   138	        event.steps === 1 ? 'step' : 'steps'
   139	      } from ${describePosition(event.from)} to ${describePosition(event.to)}.`;
   140	    case 'secret-passage':
   141	      return `${event.player} slips through the secret passage from the ${event.from} to the ${event.to}.`;
   142	    case 'suggestion-made':
   143	      return `${event.player} suggests ${event.suspect} in the ${event.room} with the ${event.weapon}.`;
   144	    case 'token-relocated':
   145	      return `${event.token} is moved to the ${event.to}.`;
   146	    case 'suggestion-refuted':
   147	      return `${event.refuter} refutes ${event.player}'s suggestion with a card shown in private.`;
   148	    case 'refutation-card-shown':
   149	      return `${event.refuter} shows you the ${event.card}.`;
   150	    case 'suggestion-unrefuted':
   151	      return `No one can refute ${event.player}'s suggestion.`;
   152	    case 'accusation-made':
   153	      return `${event.player} accuses ${event.suspect} in the ${event.room} with the ${event.weapon} — ${
   154	        event.correct ? 'and is right' : 'and is wrong'
   155	      }.`;
   156	    case 'player-eliminated':
   157	      return `${event.player} is out of the running, but must still refute suggestions.`;
   158	    case 'turn-ended':
   159	      return `${event.player}'s turn ends.`;
   160	    case 'game-over':
   161	      return event.winner === null
   162	        ? `The game ends unsolved. It was ${event.caseFile.suspect} in the ${event.caseFile.room} with the ${event.caseFile.weapon}.`
   163	        : `${event.winner} wins: ${event.caseFile.suspect} in the ${event.caseFile.room} with the ${event.caseFile.weapon}.`;
   164	  }
   165	}
   166	
   167	/** A player's whole visible history as plain sentences. */
   168	export function describeLog(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): string[] {
   169	  return visibleEvents(state, playerId).map(describeEvent);
   170	}
   171	
   172	/** Every card whose location a player has proven, and where it is. */
   173	export function knownCards(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): Map<Card, PlayerId> {
   174	  const known = new Map<Card, PlayerId>();
   175	  for (const card of handOf(state, playerId)) known.set(card, playerId);
   176	  for (const event of visibleEvents(state, playerId)) {
   177	    if (event.type === 'refutation-card-shown') known.set(event.card, event.refuter);
   178	  }
   179	  return known;
   180	}

    97	      expect(leaked).toEqual([]);
    98	
    99	      // Belt and braces: not one card of the asker's own hand beyond their token.
   100	      for (const card of FIXTURE_HANDS.p1 as Card[]) {
   101	        if (card === 'Miss Scarlett') continue;
   102	        expect(body).not.toContain(card);
   103	      }
   104	    } finally {
   105	      await vendor.stop();
   106	    }
   107	  });

     2	 * The narrator: engine events in, prose out, one batched call per turn.
     3	 *
     4	 * Narration is discardable by construction (CONTEXT.md, ADR-0001). Every test
     5	 * that breaks the model asserts the engine's own `describeEvent` sentence is
     6	 * shown VERBATIM instead, so a dead vendor costs flavor and nothing else.

    10	
    11	import { makeSuggestion, rollDice } from '../../src/engine/actions.ts';

    21	/** A real turn's worth of real events, seen by p1. */
    22	function turnEvents(): GameEvent[] {
    23	  const start = createGame({ seed: 'narrate', playerCount: 3 });
    24	  const rolled = rollDice(start);
    25	  const suggested = makeSuggestion(standingInRoom(arrangedGame(), 'p1', 'Library'), {
    26	    suspect: 'Mrs. Peacock',
    27	    weapon: 'Revolver',
    28	  });
    29	  return [...visibleEvents(rolled, 'p1'), ...visibleEvents(suggested, 'p1')].slice(0, 6);
    30	}

    32	describe('narration', () => {
    33	  test('one call per turn covers the whole batch of events', async () => {
    34	    const events = turnEvents();
    35	    const vendor = startFakeVendor(() =>
    36	      completionResponse({
    37	        content: JSON.stringify(events.map((_event, index) => `Line ${index} in a velvet voice.`)),
    38	        usage: { prompt_tokens: 80, completion_tokens: 60, total_tokens: 140 },
    39	      }),
    40	    );
    41	    try {
    42	      const client = clientFor(vendor.baseUrl);
    43	      const lines = await narrateEvents(client, { scenario: CANNED_SCENARIO, events });
    44	      console.log('[narration] lines ->', lines.map((line) => line.text));
    45	
    46	      expect(vendor.requests).toHaveLength(1);
    47	      expect(lines).toHaveLength(events.length);
    48	      lines.forEach((line, index) => {
    49	        expect(line.source).toBe('llm');
    50	        expect(line.text).toBe(`Line ${index} in a velvet voice.`);
    51	        expect(line.event).toBe(events[index]!);
    52	      });
    53	
    54	      // The prompt carries the engine's own account of each event, so the model
    55	      // is describing facts rather than inventing them.
    56	      const prompt = promptTextOf(vendor.requests[0]!);
    57	      for (const event of events) expect(prompt).toContain(describeEvent(event));
    58	      expect(client.usage.total_tokens).toBe(140);
    59	    } finally {
    60	      await vendor.stop();
    61	    }
    62	  });
    63	
    64	  test('a transport failure falls back to describeEvent, verbatim', async () => {
    65	    const events = turnEvents();
    66	    const vendor = startFakeVendor(() => errorResponse(500, 'no narrator today'));
    67	    try {
    68	      const lines = await narrateEvents(clientFor(vendor.baseUrl), {
    69	        scenario: CANNED_SCENARIO,
    70	        events,
    71	      });
    72	      console.log('[narration failed] ->', lines.map((line) => line.text));
    73	
    74	      expect(lines).toHaveLength(events.length);
    75	      lines.forEach((line, index) => {
    76	        expect(line.source).toBe('fallback');
    77	        expect(line.text).toBe(describeEvent(events[index]!));
    78	      });
    79	    } finally {
    80	      await vendor.stop();
    81	    }
    82	  });
    83	
    84	  test('a reply that is not a JSON array falls back for every event', async () => {
    85	    const events = turnEvents();

    87	      completionResponse({ content: 'The night was dark and full of nonsense.' }),
    88	    );
    89	    try {
    90	      const lines = await narrateEvents(clientFor(vendor.baseUrl), {
    91	        scenario: CANNED_SCENARIO,

     1	import { loadLlmConfig, type LlmConfig, type LoadLlmConfigOptions } from "./config.ts";
     2	import {
     3	  LlmError,
     4	  LlmHttpError,
     5	  LlmNetworkError,
     6	  LlmProtocolError,
     7	  LlmTimeoutError,
     8	} from "./errors.ts";

    10	export interface ChatMessage {
    11	  role: "system" | "user" | "assistant";
    12	  content: string;
    13	}
    14	
    15	/** The token figures exactly as the provider reported them. Never estimated. */
    16	export interface TokenUsage {
    17	  prompt_tokens: number;
    18	  completion_tokens: number;
    19	  total_tokens: number;
    20	}
    21	
    22	export interface ChatResult {
    23	  /** `choices[0].message.content`. */
    24	  text: string;
    25	  /** The response's `usage` object, or null when the provider did not report one. */
    26	  usage: TokenUsage | null;
    27	  /** The model the response attributed itself to, falling back to the model requested. */
    28	  model: string;
    29	}
    30	
    31	export type ChatOutcome =
    32	  | { ok: true; value: ChatResult }
    33	  | { ok: false; error: LlmError };
    34	
    35	export interface ChatOptions {
    36	  /** Overrides the configured model for this call only. */
    37	  model?: string;
    38	  /** Overrides the configured timeout for this call only. */
    39	  timeoutMs?: number;
    40	  /** Extra protocol parameters merged into the request body (temperature, max_tokens, stop, ...). */
    41	  params?: Record<string, unknown>;
    42	  /** Caller-owned abort signal, honoured alongside the timeout. */
    43	  signal?: AbortSignal;
    44	}
    45	
    46	export interface ModelUsage {
    47	  calls: number;
    48	  prompt_tokens: number;
    49	  completion_tokens: number;
    50	  total_tokens: number;
    51	}
    52	
    53	/**
    54	 * Running total for one client's lifetime — TOKEN-COUNT attribution v1
    55	 * (orchestrator resolution 5rwbt08h): what is accounted for is token counts per
    56	 * model, read from each response, not dollar cost.
    57	 *
    58	 * `byModel` is keyed by the LOWERCASED model id. Poe matches model ids
    59	 * case-insensitively and echoes back whatever casing the request used, so
    60	 * keying on the raw string would silently split one model's tokens across two
    61	 * buckets and understate its share.
    62	 */
    63	export interface SessionUsage {
    64	  calls: number;
    65	  callsWithUsage: number;
    66	  callsWithoutUsage: number;
    67	  prompt_tokens: number;
    68	  completion_tokens: number;
    69	  total_tokens: number;
    70	  byModel: Record<string, ModelUsage>;
    71	}
    72	
    73	/** The attribution key for a model id: case- and whitespace-insensitive. */
    74	export function usageKeyFor(model: string): string {
    75	  return model.trim().toLowerCase();
    76	}
    77	
    78	const BODY_EXCERPT_LIMIT = 500;
    79	
    80	/**
    81	 * An OpenAI-compatible chat-completions client (ADR-0002).
    82	 *
    83	 * One transport, no vendor SDK: `POST {baseUrl}/chat/completions` with a bearer
    84	 * key, a body of `{model, messages, ...params}`, and a reply read from
    85	 * `choices[0].message.content` plus the response's own `usage` object.
    86	 */
    87	export class ChatClient {
    88	  readonly config: LlmConfig;
    89	
    90	  #calls = 0;
    91	  #callsWithUsage = 0;
    92	  #promptTokens = 0;
    93	  #completionTokens = 0;
    94	  #totalTokens = 0;
    95	  readonly #byModel = new Map<string, ModelUsage>();
    96	
    97	  constructor(config: LlmConfig) {
    98	    this.config = config;
    99	  }
   100	
   101	  /** The URL every request goes to. */
   102	  get endpoint(): string {
   103	    return `${this.config.baseUrl}/chat/completions`;
   104	  }
   105	
   106	  /** A defensive copy of the running token ledger. Mutating it cannot corrupt the client. */
   107	  get usage(): SessionUsage {
   108	    const byModel: Record<string, ModelUsage> = {};
   109	    for (const [model, totals] of this.#byModel) byModel[model] = { ...totals };
   110	    return {
   111	      calls: this.#calls,
   112	      callsWithUsage: this.#callsWithUsage,
   113	      callsWithoutUsage: this.#calls - this.#callsWithUsage,
   114	      prompt_tokens: this.#promptTokens,
   115	      completion_tokens: this.#completionTokens,
   116	      total_tokens: this.#totalTokens,
   117	      byModel,
   118	    };
   119	  }
   120	
   121	  /**
   122	   * Perform one chat completion. Throws a typed `LlmError` on any failure —
   123	   * use {@link tryChat} where a failure must degrade rather than propagate.
   124	   */
   125	  async chat(messages: ChatMessage[], options: ChatOptions = {}): Promise<ChatResult> {
   126	    const model = options.model ?? this.config.model;
   127	    const timeoutMs = options.timeoutMs ?? this.config.timeoutMs;
   128	    const body = JSON.stringify({ ...(options.params ?? {}), model, messages });
   129	
   130	    const raw = await this.#send(body, timeoutMs, options.signal);
   131	    const result = parseCompletion(raw, this.endpoint, model);
   132	    this.#account(result);
   133	    return result;
   134	  }
   135	
   136	  /**
   137	   * Perform one chat completion, returning a failure instead of throwing.
   138	   *
   139	   * PRODUCT.md hard constraint 5: the game stays playable when the model
   140	   * errors, so the game loop calls this and falls back to plain engine text.
   141	   */
   142	  async tryChat(messages: ChatMessage[], options: ChatOptions = {}): Promise<ChatOutcome> {
   143	    try {
   144	      return { ok: true, value: await this.chat(messages, options) };
   145	    } catch (error) {
   146	      if (error instanceof LlmError) return { ok: false, error };
   147	      throw error;
   148	    }
   149	  }
   150	
   151	  /**
   152	   * One HTTP exchange, with a single retry on 429/5xx.
   153	   *
   154	   * A 4xx other than 429 is the caller's own bug and is never retried; a
   155	   * timeout is not retried either, because the caller's deadline has already
   156	   * passed once.
   157	   */
   158	  async #send(body: string, timeoutMs: number, callerSignal?: AbortSignal): Promise<string> {
   159	    for (let attempt = 0; ; attempt++) {
   160	      const { response, raw } = await this.#attempt(body, timeoutMs, callerSignal);
   161	      if (response.ok) return raw;
   162	
   163	      const retryable = response.status === 429 || response.status >= 500;
   164	      const excerpt = excerptOf(raw);
   165	
   166	      if (retryable && attempt === 0) {
   167	        await Bun.sleep(this.config.retryBackoffMs);
   168	        continue;
   169	      }
   170	
   171	      throw new LlmHttpError({
   172	        status: response.status,
   173	        statusText: response.statusText,
   174	        url: this.endpoint,
   175	        bodyExcerpt: excerpt,
   176	      });
   177	    }
   178	  }
   179	
   180	  async #attempt(
   181	    body: string,
   182	    timeoutMs: number,
   183	    callerSignal?: AbortSignal,
   184	  ): Promise<{ response: Response; raw: string }> {
   185	    const controller = new AbortController();
   186	    const onCallerAbort = () => controller.abort(callerSignal?.reason);
   187	    callerSignal?.addEventListener("abort", onCallerAbort, { once: true });
   188	
   189	    let timedOut = false;
   190	    const timer = setTimeout(() => {
   191	      timedOut = true;
   192	      controller.abort();
   193	    }, timeoutMs);
   194	
   195	    try {
   196	      const response = await fetch(this.endpoint, {
   197	        method: "POST",
   198	        headers: {
   199	          Authorization: `Bearer ${this.config.apiKey}`,
   200	          "Content-Type": "application/json",
   201	          Accept: "application/json",
   202	        },
   203	        body,
   204	        signal: controller.signal,
   205	      });
   206	      // The body is read INSIDE the armed window: a vendor that sends headers
   207	      // and then stalls the stream is a timeout, not a hang (panel critique,
   208	      // drive kqrr2q4q). The same abort covers both halves of the exchange.
   209	      const raw = await response.text();
   210	      return { response, raw };
   211	    } catch (cause) {
   212	      if (timedOut) {
   213	        throw new LlmTimeoutError({ baseUrl: this.config.baseUrl, timeoutMs });
   214	      }
   215	      if (callerSignal?.aborted) throw cause;
   216	      throw new LlmNetworkError({ baseUrl: this.config.baseUrl, cause });
   217	    } finally {
   218	      clearTimeout(timer);
   219	      callerSignal?.removeEventListener("abort", onCallerAbort);
   220	    }
   221	  }
   222	
   223	  #account(result: ChatResult): void {
   224	    this.#calls += 1;
   225	    if (result.usage === null) return;
   226	
   227	    this.#callsWithUsage += 1;
   228	    this.#promptTokens += result.usage.prompt_tokens;
   229	    this.#completionTokens += result.usage.completion_tokens;
   230	    this.#totalTokens += result.usage.total_tokens;
   231	
   232	    const key = usageKeyFor(result.model);
   233	    const totals = this.#byModel.get(key) ?? {
   234	      calls: 0,
   235	      prompt_tokens: 0,
   236	      completion_tokens: 0,
   237	      total_tokens: 0,
   238	    };
   239	    totals.calls += 1;
   240	    totals.prompt_tokens += result.usage.prompt_tokens;
   241	    totals.completion_tokens += result.usage.completion_tokens;
   242	    totals.total_tokens += result.usage.total_tokens;
   243	    this.#byModel.set(key, totals);
   244	  }
   245	}
   246	
   247	/** Build a client straight from the environment and `.env.local`. */
   248	export function createChatClient(options: LoadLlmConfigOptions = {}): ChatClient {
   249	  return new ChatClient(loadLlmConfig(options));
   250	}
   251	
   252	/** Read `choices[0].message.content` and the optional `usage` object out of a raw response body. */
   253	export function parseCompletion(raw: string, url: string, requestedModel: string): ChatResult {
   254	  let payload: unknown;
   255	  try {
   256	    payload = JSON.parse(raw);
   257	  } catch {
   258	    throw new LlmProtocolError({ url, reason: "response body is not JSON", bodyExcerpt: excerptOf(raw) });
   259	  }
   260	
   261	  if (typeof payload !== "object" || payload === null) {
   262	    throw new LlmProtocolError({ url, reason: "response body is not an object", bodyExcerpt: excerptOf(raw) });
   263	  }
   264	
   265	  const record = payload as Record<string, unknown>;
   266	  const choices = record.choices;
   267	  if (!Array.isArray(choices) || choices.length === 0) {
   268	    throw new LlmProtocolError({ url, reason: "no choices in response", bodyExcerpt: excerptOf(raw) });
   269	  }
   270	
   271	  const message = (choices[0] as Record<string, unknown> | undefined)?.message as
   272	    | Record<string, unknown>
   273	    | undefined;
   274	  const text = message?.content;
   275	  if (typeof text !== "string") {
   276	    throw new LlmProtocolError({
   277	      url,
   278	      reason: "choices[0].message.content is not a string",
   279	      bodyExcerpt: excerptOf(raw),
   280	    });
   281	  }
   282	
   283	  const model = typeof record.model === "string" && record.model.length > 0 ? record.model : requestedModel;
   284	
   285	  return { text, usage: parseUsage(record.usage), model };
   286	}
   287	
   288	/**
   289	 * Read the provider's `usage` object.
   290	 *
   291	 * Absent or unusable usage yields null — tolerated, per ADR-0002, because not
   292	 * every OpenAI-compatible host reports it. A partial object is NEVER
   293	 * zero-filled (panel critique, drive kqrr2q4q — fabricated counts corrupt the
   294	 * token ledger): the third field is derived exactly when two are present
   295	 * (prompt + completion = total), and a lone field is no usage at all.
   296	 */
   297	export function parseUsage(value: unknown): TokenUsage | null {
   298	  if (typeof value !== "object" || value === null) return null;
   299	  const record = value as Record<string, unknown>;
   300	
   301	  const prompt = numberOrNull(record.prompt_tokens);
   302	  const completion = numberOrNull(record.completion_tokens);
   303	  const total = numberOrNull(record.total_tokens);
   304	
   305	  if (prompt !== null && completion !== null) {
   306	    return { prompt_tokens: prompt, completion_tokens: completion, total_tokens: total ?? prompt + completion };
   307	  }
   308	  if (total !== null && prompt !== null) {
   309	    return { prompt_tokens: prompt, completion_tokens: total - prompt, total_tokens: total };
   310	  }
   311	  if (total !== null && completion !== null) {
   312	    return { prompt_tokens: total - completion, completion_tokens: completion, total_tokens: total };
   313	  }
   314	  return null;
   315	}
   316	
   317	function numberOrNull(value: unknown): number | null {
   318	  return typeof value === "number" && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null;
   319	}
   320	
   321	function excerptOf(body: string): string {
   322	  const trimmed = body.trim();
   323	  return trimmed.length > BODY_EXCERPT_LIMIT
   324	    ? `${trimmed.slice(0, BODY_EXCERPT_LIMIT)}… (truncated)`
   325	    : trimmed;
   326	}
     1	/**
     2	 * The chat client: the project's only LLM transport (ADR-0002).
     3	 *

     1	/**
     2	 * Projections of game state for consumers that must NOT see everything.
     3	 *
     4	 * The CLI and the LLM game master are both untrusted with the case file and
     5	 * with other players' hands (ADR-0001: leaking either into a prompt would spoil
     6	 * the puzzle). They read a `PlayerView`, never `GameState`.
     7	 *
     8	 * `describeEvent` is also the narration fallback: when the game master returns
     9	 * nothing or errors, the engine's own plain sentence is shown instead.
    10	 */
    11	
    12	import type { Card, Room, Suspect, Weapon } from './cards.ts';
    13	import type { Position } from './board.ts';
    14	import type { GameEvent, GameState, PlayerId, TurnPhase } from './types.ts';
    15	import { IllegalActionError } from './types.ts';
    16	
    17	/** Events a given player is entitled to see, in order. */
    18	export function visibleEvents(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): GameEvent[] {
    19	  return state.events.filter(
    20	    (event) => event.visibleTo === 'all' || event.visibleTo.includes(playerId),
    21	  );
    22	}
    23	
    24	/** Events every player can see. Safe to show to a spectator or a narrator. */
    25	export function publicEvents(state: GameState): GameEvent[] {
    26	  return state.events.filter((event) => event.visibleTo === 'all');
    27	}
    28	
    29	/** The cards a player holds. Throws for an unknown player. */
    30	export function handOf(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): readonly Card[] {
    31	  const player = state.players.find((candidate) => candidate.id === playerId);
    32	  if (!player) throw new IllegalActionError(`no such player: ${playerId}`);
    33	  return player.hand;
    34	}
    35	
    36	export type PlayerView = {
    37	  readonly you: PlayerId;
    38	  readonly character: Suspect;
    39	  readonly hand: readonly Card[];
    40	  readonly position: Position;
    41	  readonly eliminated: boolean;
    42	  readonly phase: TurnPhase;
    43	  readonly roll: number | null;
    44	  readonly currentPlayer: PlayerId;
    45	  readonly yourTurn: boolean;
    46	  readonly turnNumber: number;
    47	  readonly over: boolean;
    48	  readonly winner: PlayerId | null;
    49	  /** Public facts about the other seats: no hands, only hand SIZES. */
    50	  readonly opponents: readonly {
    51	    readonly id: PlayerId;
    52	    readonly character: Suspect;
    53	    readonly handSize: number;
    54	    readonly eliminated: boolean;
    55	    readonly position: Position;
    56	  }[];
    57	  readonly suspectPositions: Readonly<Record<Suspect, Position>>;
    58	  readonly weaponPositions: Readonly<Record<Weapon, Room>>;
    59	  readonly events: readonly GameEvent[];
    60	  /**
    61	   * The refutation the game is waiting on, or null. Everyone may know WHO owes
    62	   * the choice — it is why play has stopped — but the cards on offer are part
    63	   * of the refuter's hand, so `options` is filled in for that player alone.
    64	   */
    65	  readonly pendingRefutation: {
    66	    readonly suggester: PlayerId;
    67	    readonly refuter: PlayerId;
    68	    readonly yours: boolean;
    69	    readonly options: readonly Card[] | null;
    70	  } | null;
    71	};
    72	
    73	/**
    74	 * Everything one player may know: their own hand, public board state, and the
    75	 * events addressed to them. The case file is absent by construction, and so is
    76	 * every other hand.
    77	 */
    78	export function playerView(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): PlayerView {
    79	  const player = state.players.find((candidate) => candidate.id === playerId);
    80	  if (!player) throw new IllegalActionError(`no such player: ${playerId}`);
    81	  const current = state.players[state.currentPlayerIndex];
    82	  return {
    83	    you: player.id,
    84	    character: player.character,
    85	    hand: player.hand,
    86	    position: state.suspectPositions[player.character],
    87	    eliminated: player.eliminated,
    88	    phase: state.phase,
    89	    roll: state.roll,
    90	    currentPlayer: current?.id ?? player.id,
    91	    yourTurn: current?.id === player.id,
    92	    turnNumber: state.turnNumber,
    93	    over: state.over,
    94	    winner: state.winner,
    95	    opponents: state.players
    96	      .filter((candidate) => candidate.id !== playerId)
    97	      .map((candidate) => ({
    98	        id: candidate.id,
    99	        character: candidate.character,
   100	        handSize: candidate.hand.length,
   101	        eliminated: candidate.eliminated,
   102	        position: state.suspectPositions[candidate.character],
   103	      })),
   104	    suspectPositions: state.suspectPositions,
   105	    weaponPositions: state.weaponPositions,
   106	    events: visibleEvents(state, playerId),
   107	    pendingRefutation:
   108	      state.pendingRefutation === null
   109	        ? null
   110	        : {
   111	            suggester: state.pendingRefutation.suggester,
   112	            refuter: state.pendingRefutation.refuter,
   113	            yours: state.pendingRefutation.refuter === playerId,
   114	            options:
   115	              state.pendingRefutation.refuter === playerId ? state.pendingRefutation.options : null,
   116	          },
   117	  };
   118	}
   119	
   120	/** A position as a short human phrase, e.g. "the Library" or "corridor 6,7". */
   121	export function describePosition(position: Position): string {
   122	  return position.kind === 'room' ? `the ${position.room}` : `corridor ${position.x},${position.y}`;
   123	}
   124	
   125	/** One event as a plain English sentence — the narration fallback. */
   126	export function describeEvent(event: GameEvent): string {
   127	  switch (event.type) {
   128	    case 'game-started':
   129	      return `The game begins with ${event.players
   130	        .map((entry) => `${entry.id} as ${entry.character}`)
   131	        .join(', ')}.`;
   132	    case 'turn-started':
   133	      return `Turn ${event.turn}: it is ${event.player}'s move.`;
   134	    case 'rolled':
   135	      return `${event.player} rolls a ${event.value}.`;
   136	    case 'moved':
   137	      return `${event.player} moves ${event.steps} ${
   138	        event.steps === 1 ? 'step' : 'steps'
   139	      } from ${describePosition(event.from)} to ${describePosition(event.to)}.`;
   140	    case 'secret-passage':
   141	      return `${event.player} slips through the secret passage from the ${event.from} to the ${event.to}.`;
   142	    case 'suggestion-made':
   143	      return `${event.player} suggests ${event.suspect} in the ${event.room} with the ${event.weapon}.`;
   144	    case 'token-relocated':
   145	      return `${event.token} is moved to the ${event.to}.`;
   146	    case 'suggestion-refuted':
   147	      return `${event.refuter} refutes ${event.player}'s suggestion with a card shown in private.`;
   148	    case 'refutation-card-shown':
   149	      return `${event.refuter} shows you the ${event.card}.`;
   150	    case 'suggestion-unrefuted':
   151	      return `No one can refute ${event.player}'s suggestion.`;
   152	    case 'accusation-made':
   153	      return `${event.player} accuses ${event.suspect} in the ${event.room} with the ${event.weapon} — ${
   154	        event.correct ? 'and is right' : 'and is wrong'
   155	      }.`;
   156	    case 'player-eliminated':
   157	      return `${event.player} is out of the running, but must still refute suggestions.`;
   158	    case 'turn-ended':
   159	      return `${event.player}'s turn ends.`;
   160	    case 'game-over':
   161	      return event.winner === null
   162	        ? `The game ends unsolved. It was ${event.caseFile.suspect} in the ${event.caseFile.room} with the ${event.caseFile.weapon}.`
   163	        : `${event.winner} wins: ${event.caseFile.suspect} in the ${event.caseFile.room} with the ${event.caseFile.weapon}.`;
   164	  }
   165	}
   166	
   167	/** A player's whole visible history as plain sentences. */
   168	export function describeLog(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): string[] {
   169	  return visibleEvents(state, playerId).map(describeEvent);
   170	}
   171	
   172	/** Every card whose location a player has proven, and where it is. */
   173	export function knownCards(state: GameState, playerId: PlayerId): Map<Card, PlayerId> {
   174	  const known = new Map<Card, PlayerId>();
   175	  for (const card of handOf(state, playerId)) known.set(card, playerId);
   176	  for (const event of visibleEvents(state, playerId)) {
   177	    if (event.type === 'refutation-card-shown') known.set(event.card, event.refuter);
   178	  }
   179	  return known;
   180	}

     1	/**
     2	 * The LLM game master.
     3	 *
     4	 * Scenario, narration and in-fiction Q&A, layered over engine-decided facts —
     5	 * plus the deterministic opponent policy that plays the suspects the human is
     6	 * not. The model describes; it never decides (ADR-0001).
     7	 *
     8	 * Everything goes through ONE `ChatClient`, which is what makes the session's
     9	 * token ledger honest: the roles may run on different models, and the ledger
    10	 * attributes each call's `usage` figures to the model that reported them
    11	 * (PRODUCT.md hard constraint 6, orchestrator resolution 5rwbt08h).
    12	 */
    13	
    14	import type { Suspect } from '../engine/cards.ts';
    15	import type { GameEvent } from '../engine/types.ts';
    16	import type { PlayerView } from '../engine/view.ts';
    17	import type { ChatClient, SessionUsage } from '../llm/index.ts';
    18	import { CANNED_SCENARIO, requestScenario, type Scenario } from './scenario.ts';
    19	import { narrateEvents, type NarrationLine } from './narrator.ts';
    20	import { askSuspect, type SuspectAnswer } from './qa.ts';
    21	
    22	export {
    23	  CANNED_SCENARIO,
    24	  parseScenario,
    25	  requestScenario,
    26	  scenarioMessages,
    27	  type Scenario,
    28	  type ScenarioOptions,
    29	} from './scenario.ts';
    30	export {
    31	  fallbackNarration,
    32	  narrateEvents,
    33	  narrationMessages,
    34	  parseNarration,
    35	  type NarrationLine,
    36	  type NarrationRequest,
    37	} from './narrator.ts';
    38	export {
    39	  askSuspect,
    40	  fallbackAnswer,
    41	  questionMessages,
    42	  visibleFactsFor,
    43	  type QuestionRequest,
    44	  type SuspectAnswer,
    45	  type VisibleFacts,

    54	} from './opponent.ts';

    56	export type GameMasterOptions = {
    57	  /** Per-role model overrides. Each defaults to the client's configured model. */
    58	  readonly models?: {
    59	    readonly scenario?: string;
    60	    readonly narration?: string;
    61	    readonly qa?: string;
    62	  };
    63	};
    64	
    65	export class GameMaster {
    66	  readonly client: ChatClient;
    67	  readonly models: NonNullable<GameMasterOptions['models']>;
    68	
    69	  #scenario: Scenario | null = null;
    70	
    71	  constructor(client: ChatClient, options: GameMasterOptions = {}) {
    72	    this.client = client;
    73	    this.models = options.models ?? {};
    74	  }
    75	
    76	  /** The session's token ledger, straight from the shared client. */
    77	  get usage(): SessionUsage {
    78	    return this.client.usage;
    79	  }
    80	
    81	  /** The scenario opened for this session, or null before `openScenario`. */
    82	  get scenario(): Scenario | null {
    83	    return this.#scenario;
    84	  }
    85	
    86	  /**
    87	   * Generate the session's scenario, once. Repeat calls return the same value
    88	   * without spending another token — the fiction is fixed for the session.
    89	   */
    90	  async openScenario(): Promise<Scenario> {
    91	    if (this.#scenario !== null) return this.#scenario;
    92	    this.#scenario = await requestScenario(this.client, {
    93	      ...(this.models.scenario === undefined ? {} : { model: this.models.scenario }),
    94	    });
    95	    return this.#scenario;
    96	  }
    97	
    98	  /** Narrate a turn's events, one batched call, falling back per event. */
    99	  async narrate(events: readonly GameEvent[]): Promise<NarrationLine[]> {
   100	    return narrateEvents(this.client, {
   101	      scenario: this.#scenario ?? CANNED_SCENARIO,
   102	      events,
   103	      ...(this.models.narration === undefined ? {} : { model: this.models.narration }),
   104	    });
   105	  }
   106	
   107	  /** Put one in-fiction question to one suspect, from the asker's view alone. */
   108	  async ask(view: PlayerView, suspect: Suspect, question: string): Promise<SuspectAnswer> {
   109	    return askSuspect(this.client, {
   110	      scenario: this.#scenario ?? CANNED_SCENARIO,
   111	      view,
   112	      suspect,
   113	      question,
   114	      ...(this.models.qa === undefined ? {} : { model: this.models.qa }),
   115	    });
   116	  }
   117	}
   118	
   119	/**
   120	 * The session's token ledger as printable lines — measured from each response's
   121	 * own `usage` object, never estimated from string length (ADR-0002).
   122	 *
   123	 * Calls a provider reported no usage for are stated rather than hidden: a
   124	 * ledger that silently drops them would understate the session.
   125	 */
   126	export function formatUsageLedger(usage: SessionUsage): string[] {
   127	  if (usage.calls === 0) return ['No LLM calls were made this session.'];
   128	
   129	  const lines = [
   130	    `LLM usage: ${usage.calls} ${usage.calls === 1 ? 'call' : 'calls'}, ` +
   131	      `${usage.total_tokens} tokens (${usage.prompt_tokens} prompt + ${usage.completion_tokens} completion)`,
   132	  ];
   133	  for (const model of Object.keys(usage.byModel).sort()) {
   134	    const totals = usage.byModel[model];
   135	    if (totals === undefined) continue;
   136	    lines.push(
   137	      `  ${model}: ${totals.calls} ${totals.calls === 1 ? 'call' : 'calls'}, ` +
   138	        `${totals.total_tokens} tokens (${totals.prompt_tokens} prompt + ${totals.completion_tokens} completion)`,
   139	    );
   140	  }
   141	  if (usage.callsWithoutUsage > 0) {
   142	    lines.push(
   143	      `  ${usage.callsWithoutUsage} ${usage.callsWithoutUsage === 1 ? 'call' : 'calls'} reported no usage and ${
   144	        usage.callsWithoutUsage === 1 ? 'is' : 'are'
   145	      } not counted above`,
   146	    );
   147	  }
   148	  return lines;
   149	}
